Integration startup deadline review: module entry/discovery previously had no host deadline, so a cooperative stalled adapter could leave startup pending despite tool-call timeouts. Installation now validates timeout as a finite non-boolean number greater than zero and at most 60 seconds, default 10, before any module construction. Entry and registration/identity discovery share that per-module deadline. Expiry reports integration_startup_timeout; the existing exit stack releases successfully entered adapters and failed entry retains the adapter's own partial-cleanup responsibility. Cancellation cannot preempt synchronous blocking or cancellation-suppressing trusted Python code; this is not a hard process-isolation or shutdown deadline guarantee.
`uv run --extra vision --extra robot --extra robot-camera iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_integration_startup_deadline' --output local-data/evidence/stage-integration-startup-deadline-before` retained nine failures. Initial fixed subset passed 38 checks; Ruff flagged a test parameter named timeout, then the parameter was renamed without changing assertions. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot --extra robot-camera iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_integration_startup_deadline or test_module_installation or test_direct_startup_credentials or test_direct_adapter or actual_calendar_transports' --output local-data/evidence/stage-integration-startup-deadline-reviewed` passed 37 checks, including actual local stdio/Streamable HTTP with fake calendar stores. This closes an independent startup defect while the confirmed real wave/thumb and human K1 prerequisites stay blocked with unchanged thresholds. Latest full regression/package predates the two startup fixes; no physical or provider qualification promoted.

Latest milestone: bound document revision hashing during file growth. _revision previously checked stat then hashed to EOF, allowing a file growing after stat to exceed the specified 20 MiB read limit. Exact final count/hash command (PowerShell; preparation integrity only):

```powershell
$manifest = Get-Content -Raw docs/iago/FEATURES.json | ConvertFrom-Json
$hash = (Get-FileHash docs/iago/SPEC.md -Algorithm SHA256).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()
if ($manifest.source_sha256 -ne $hash) { throw 'Specification hash mismatch' }
$goal = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllText((Join-Path (Get-Location) 'docs/iago/GOAL.txt'))
$objective = $goal.Substring(6).TrimEnd([char]10,[char]13)
if ($objective.Length -gt 4000) { throw 'Goal too long' }
Write-Output "Features=$($manifest.features.Count); Objective=$($objective.Length); Command=$($goal.Length); SHA256=$hash"
```

Observed: Features=36; Objective=2508; Command=2515; SHA256=f693ec33b066723946fcb777f2817da467fbd72165db1eb7af2bf0cad9324f8e. These are planning-integrity results only. No application build, test harness, acceptance pass, provider connection or build-goal activation occurred.

- Added the real backend edge client with authenticated WebSocket control/microphone channels, bounded HTTP snapshot streaming, TLS verification for remote connections, ownership IDs and heartbeat processing. Interrupted control requests close the connection so an orphan reply cannot be consumed by a later command. No credentials are exposed to model or browser settings.
- Added round-trip clock-offset estimation with uncertainty and expiry, microphone sequence-gap reporting, and explicit unknown capture timing for SDK snapshots. These estimates do not establish robot timing/physical qualification.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot pytest tests/test_edge_clock.py -q` passed 4 checks. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot pytest tests/test_edge_transport.py -q` passed a real loopback HTTP/WebSocket client/server test with fake media, including mode/authorization/Stop, stale authorization rejection, snapshot bytes, clock estimate and actual serving-thread cleanup.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-edge-client` passed 81 checks, 3 provider checks outside the selector. Ruff passed. Robot deployment composition is not yet complete: the primary launcher still explicitly rejects robot profiles. Next work must wire this client into persistent robot conversation ownership and both robot profiles, then complete the remaining full-scope requirements and qualifications.

### Build milestone: ordered robot audio commits

- Previous turn was progress (persistent robot profile composition and page-ownership evidence). Re-inspected the edge microphone and backend transcription paths; identified that a heartbeat speech-end marker could arrive before its microphone audio.
- Local VAD now attaches sequence-associated speech events and capture-time estimates to the same ordered microphone packets. The backend commits only an end marker received after appending that packet's audio. Earlier control-channel copies do not commit; duplicate markers are deduplicated. Speech-onset estimates are mapped through the edge clock and unresolved timing invalidates gesture eligibility. Local stop remains independent of PC handling.
- Added a synthetic cross-channel ordering case. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot pytest tests/test_robot_audio_order.py tests/test_edge_supervisor.py tests/test_presented_question.py -q` passed 5 checks. The full offline regression command is `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-robot-audio-order`; its generated report is authoritative for the result. Ruff passed.
- Inspected installed camera read implementation: its public JPEG helper is intended for occasional stills and discards sample timing. Continuous robot perception must use a separate paced raw-frame/preview path; do not advertise the current one-fps snapshot loop as full-rate detection or invent capture timestamps. This is the next robot implementation gap, alongside behavior dispatch/settings and all remaining full-scope work.
- The first full audio-order regression failed one existing perception-duration assertion (`seconds` was 0.0), with 83 checks passing. Short processing intervals were measured with the Windows monotonic clock. Switched elapsed-work diagnostics to `time.perf_counter()`; retained the positive-duration assertion and all acceptance requirements. The corrected regression report is `local-data/evidence/stage-robot-audio-order-fixed/offline.json`.
- Confirmed clock implementations programmatically: monotonic uses GetTickCount64 with 0.015625-second resolution; perf_counter uses QueryPerformanceCounter with 1e-07-second resolution. The corrected full run passed 84 checks, with 3 provider checks outside the selector. Physical media timing remains unqualified.

### Build milestone: robot transition failure cleanup and acknowledgment ownership

- Previous goal turn was progress (continuous camera and independent preview routing). Re-inspected current session/client/service lifecycle and repository instructions. Identified that recognition startup failure could leave the previous edge capture mode enabled.
- Mode transitions now stop the previous answer and release edge media before provider setup. Provider failures and cancellation cancel old session tasks, close partial STT, attempt edge Idle, disable visual sources and invalidate questions, and report Idle. Failed edge handshakes close transport resources; heartbeat callbacks cannot await their own task during close. Startup edge Idle failure now enters runtime cleanup.
- Only edge-origin playback and speech observations may update robot heard context/speech arbitration; control-page claims are ignored in robot profiles. Extended both profile transport tests to verify this. The edge publishes a new mode only after SDK acquisition succeeds, and retains old ownership until asynchronous media release finishes so a replacement cannot be released by stale cleanup.
- Added injected provider failure and cancellation cases without billable calls. Initial profile checks failed because they assumed exactly one Stop before state; transitions now deliberately stop before provider setup as well. Updated the protocol test to allow bounded Stop notifications before the required Aware state, retaining session survival/reattachment assertions. An over-broad test text edit briefly affected reconnect assertions; corrected it transparently before reporting passes.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-robot-lifecycle` passed 89 checks with 3 provider checks outside the selector. Subsequent ownership review and page-claim assertions were checked by `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select "robot_lifecycle or robot_profile or edge_service or edge_transport" --output local-data/evidence/stage-robot-lifecycle-review`: 6 passed, 86 outside the selector. Ruff passed. These remain partial synthetic/transport checks, not physical or whole-feature acceptance.
- Next: complete reconnect handling and robot controls/motion, backend startup failure cleanup, behavior dispatch/configuration and all remaining independent V1 implementation and required datasets. No scope or acceptance threshold was reduced.

### Build milestone: recognition stream-loss recovery

- Previous turn was progress (test-event control and Aware transport verification). Re-inspected recognition receivers and found that unexpected clean EOF was ignored, while PC exceptions only stopped the current answer without closing recognition or changing the listening state.
- PC stream loss now handles both EOF and exceptions, detaches the failed provider before cleanup, stops/clears the conversation into Aware, reports loss, and closes recognition. Ownership checks prevent a stale receiver from clearing a replacement. Recognition receiver creation follows the mode transition, avoiding an immediate EOF racing startup back into Conversation. close_stt detaches first and does not cancel/await its own receiver task.
- Robot transcription EOF now invokes the existing failure recovery path. No automatic unlimited reconnect loop or silent transcript/model substitution was added.

### Build milestone: shared transcription append/commit serialization

- Previous turn was progress (ordered robot Finish requests). Re-inspected the actual transcription adapter; appends and control-channel commits had no shared ordering lock or buffer accounting.
- Added one input lock and scalar byte accounting. A commit waits for an ongoing append; successful commit resets the count, and another commit without intervening audio is suppressed. Empty appends are ignored. Canceled/failed sends retire the transport because delivery may be uncertain; the adapter does not replay them.
- Checked official OpenAI client-event reference (https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/realtime-client-events): input_audio_buffer.commit errors on an empty buffer, creates the transcription input and clears the input buffer. This change does not assert an undocumented minimum-duration threshold or pad real input with fabricated audio. Very short nonempty input remains subject to genuine provider validation.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select "transcription_order or recognition_recovery or robot_audio or trigger" --output local-data/evidence/stage-transcription-order` passed 10 affected checks, 100 outside the selector. New injected-socket tests cover a paused append with concurrent duplicate commits, exact send order, buffer accounting and canceled-send retirement without replay. Ruff and diff checks passed. No billable API call or physical pass occurred.
- Remaining work includes PC capture-to-commit channel barriers, short-input behavior under actual provider validation, robot motion/camera/reconnect, event evidence, settings/exports/diagnostics and all full-scope acceptance datasets and physical gates. No feature is promoted to fully validated.

### Build milestone: page release despite edge failure

- Previous turn was progress (independent robot camera controls). Re-inspected disconnect cleanup and confirmed that an edge stop exception could bypass releasing page ownership, or stop clearing after the first page-owned source.

### Build milestone: bounded robot reconnection

### Build milestone: transcript correction and settings races

- Previous turn was progress (recording/UI and 126 offline passes). Re-inspected recorder admission, deletion and cancellation. Saved gestures now reserve bounded correction capacity; a correction can displace queued ordinary text when necessary, with an unsaved counter, and cannot displace another correction. Retired gesture slots release unused reservations. Corrections remain allowed after saving is disabled and address only their globally unique session/entry IDs, avoiding a setting-generation change leaving a superseded response saved.
- Settings operations keep admission paused until the thread's actual database result is known. Caller cancellation, including repeated cancellation, is propagated only after updating cached state from that result; an AnyIO shield protects this cleanup under ASGI cancellation. Shutdown clears residual queued text after bounded draining. Review also preserved unfinished-work accounting while replacing a queued item, so join cannot observe a transient completed queue.

### Build milestone: journal I/O independent of immediate Stop

- Previous turn was progress (K1 browser controls/query set). Re-read E1 durable-action and local-stop invariants. Journal calls were synchronous on the application event loop. Moved execution/reconciliation/status journal I/O to worker threads and serialized the shared SQLite connection with a lock. Immediate Stop still invalidates audio and cancels the answer without waiting for journal completion.
- Cancellation resolves the actual in-flight SQLite outcome before classifying the write. After acquiring a durable dispatch record, the executor rechecks current context, account generation and exact authorization binding before entering the provider. An acquired record canceled before provider invocation becomes canceled-before-dispatch. Known terminal outcomes cannot be overwritten by later cancellation/uncertainty bookkeeping. These changes preserve the existing journal's conservative restart handling; they do not yet implement every required proposed/queued/reconciling state.
- Executor shutdown cancels and drains active executions before integration/journal teardown, with an explicit bounded shutdown failure if tasks do not finish. Closed executors reject new work. This prevents normal cancellation cleanup from racing a closed journal connection.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select "journal_interruption or integrations or backend_lifecycle or mcp" --output local-data/evidence/stage-journal-interruption-reviewed` passed 11 affected checks, including actual local MCP transport tests. New synthetic-provider cases hold journal begin or successful finish, verify core Stop completes within a 100 ms test timeout, wait for shutdown fencing, and assert respectively zero provider mutation/canceled-before-dispatch or one mutation/preserved success. This is deterministic concurrency evidence, not physical cutoff qualification.
- After rejecting post-close execution, `uv run --extra vision --extra robot pytest tests/test_journal_interruption.py tests/test_backend_lifecycle.py -q --tb=short` passed 5 focused checks. Ruff/import formatting and diff checks passed; all 36 IDs and the specification hash remain intact. Next E1 work: full durable pre-dispatch/reconciling lifecycle, restored-account reconciliation, uncertain-operation controls and retention/idempotency matrices. Other mandatory product/dataset/physical work remains in scope; no full E1 or release validation is claimed.


### Build milestone: owned durable reconciliation

- Reconciliation uses the executor's bounded slots and shutdown tracking, rechecks account/context after claiming, and validates bounded status/reference results. ### Build milestone: durable awaiting-confirmation integration

- Previous turn was progress (durable executor queue). Made proposal creation asynchronous and wired the conversation/runtime callers to await it before presenting confirmation. Valid write proposals persist proposed then awaiting-confirmation states, recheck active context/account/binding after I/O, and fail closed if interrupted. Exact approved proposals advance the existing record to queued and dispatched; no second operation record or fresh authorization prompt is introduced.
- Rejection, expiry and turn cancellation remove the in-memory proposal immediately and schedule conditional durable cancellation. Pending proposal plus cancellation work remains bounded, and shutdown drains cancellation work before journal teardown. Stop remains independent of the database. Standing authorization superseding a pending proposal discards the old in-memory prompt when queueing the newly authorized action.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select "integrations or operation_states or journal_interruption or runtime or mcp or backend_lifecycle" --output local-data/evidence/stage-durable-proposals` passed 25 affected checks. Added awaited-state assertions, durable Stop/expiry/rejection cases, payload absence from journal bytes, and cancellation while proposal persistence is held. The existing fake calendar workflow uses the new awaited proposal path. The altered-payload confirmation test now expects operation_conflict rather than confirmation_required because durable operation identity rejects the reuse earlier; the no-mutation and exact authorized-payload assertions remain unchanged.
- Ruff and diff checks passed. EXTENDING documents awaited proposal creation, cancellation and restart semantics. All 36 feature IDs and the specification hash remain intact. Remaining E1 work: restored-account reconciliation authorization, explicit operation/reconciliation/cancel controls, full retention/capacity/idempotency matrices and regression review. All other incomplete V1 product/dataset/provider/physical requirements remain active; this milestone does not establish complete E1 or release validation.


### Build milestone: MCP reconciliation through separately authorized reads

- Previous turn was progress (durable awaiting-confirmation integration). Inspection found that fake-calendar operation_status existed but was not wired into journal reconciliation, and generic callback reconciliation lacked a separate read-policy gate. Replaced that callback path with a namespaced read-tool link. Reconciliation checks same module/account, enabled read tool, read scopes/capabilities, input schema and an explicit allow policy; it does not require re-enabling the original write tool.
- MCP registration now reads reconcile_tool metadata; the example calendar links create_event to operation_status. The executor invokes the actual registered read handler under its slot/timeout controls, validates its bounded output and persists only normalized status/reference fields. Queue waiting is included in the reconciliation timeout. No conversation-core change is required to add this link.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select "integrations or runtime or mcp or journal_interruption" --output local-data/evidence/stage-mcp-reconciliation-policy` passed 21 affected checks. A real stdio MCP case creates a synthetic event with deliberate response loss, verifies uncertainty under denied/disabled status access, then confirms success through the permitted read while write access remains disabled. The fake store contains exactly one event. Direct reconciliation fixtures now register read tools under policy rather than bypassing it with callbacks.
- Ruff flagged blocking fixture configuration I/O inside the new async test; moved it off-thread, reran Ruff successfully, and `uv run --extra vision --extra robot pytest tests/test_runtime.py -q --tb=short` passed both runtime cases. Diff checks, 36 IDs and the specification hash passed. EXTENDING records the link contract and limits.
- Remaining material issue: account restoration still needs a stable verified/configuration identity contract; generation equality alone cannot prove account identity across application restart or changed connector configuration. Reconnection remains conservative across generation changes. Continue this before claiming restored-account reconciliation, plus operation/cancel controls, retention/idempotency matrices and all remaining V1 qualification work. No real personal connector was connected and no full E1/product validation is claimed.


### Build milestone: account/resource identity for uncertain-write reconciliation

Prepare/dispatch compare account references, and reconciliation rechecks identity before and after provider lookup.
- The explicitly installed fake-calendar module can probe its allowed read-only identity tool. Its database retains a resource UUID; registration validates bounded issuer/resource/account fields. Same-store/account reconnection permits a separately authorized status read across connection generations. Different stores/accounts and unknown-identity reconnects preserve uncertainty. This trusted fake-server contract is not authentication of arbitrary MCP servers or production account integration.

Removed that accidental argument without changing the original assertion or acceptance criteria.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-account-identity --select "test_integrations or test_runtime or test_mcp or test_operation_states or test_journal"` passed 24 affected checks. Added a further deterministic confirmation/identity-change check for the full regression run.
- Updated EXTENDING and the fake installation configuration. E1 remains partial. Remaining independent work includes operation controls, retention/capacity/idempotency coverage, connection/direct-adapter completion, mandatory acceptance inventory and the full 36-feature implementation review. Physical and dataset/provider qualification remains separate.

- Final regression: `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-account-identity-regression` passed 160 currently implemented offline checks. Ruff over `reachy_brain tests` and `git diff --check` passed. This is the current runnable suite, not evidence that every mandatory acceptance gate has been implemented or qualified. Specification hash and all 36 unique IDs remain intact.


### Build milestone: operation status, local cancellation and reconciliation controls

- Previous turn was progress (account-bound reconciliation; 160 current offline checks passed). Ruff over reachy_brain/tests and diff checks passed. All 36 unique IDs and the specification hash remain intact.

### Build milestone: configured journal retention and automatic cleanup

- Previous turn was progress (operation controls and 30 affected checks). Added validated private settings for terminal retention (30 days), main-database admission capacity (10 MiB), and cleanup interval (one hour). Startup applies cleanup before integration use; a lifecycle-owned periodic worker performs cleanup off the event loop and drains any in-flight cleanup before database closure. Cleanup compacts only when records were removed and preserves unresolved records. Failures produce bounded sanitized diagnostics.
- Operation controls now display storage/admission status and unresolved count. Added `examples/journal.env.example` and deployment/extension guidance, including deduplication expiry after terminal records are removed. Fixed negative/invalid recent-record limits so they cannot become unbounded SQLite queries.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-journal-retention --select "operation_retention or operation_web or operation_states or integrations or journal_interruption or backend_lifecycle or test_browser"` passed 26 affected checks. New tests fill a real synthetic SQLite journal to admission capacity, verify exact retention cutoff, reclaim eligible terminal storage, retain unresolved provider references and reject duplicate unresolved dispatch. The HTTP startup test verifies configured limits and retention with old terminal/uncertain records. Existing headless browser and journal-interruption checks passed.
- Added a running-app timer check. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-journal-retention-reviewed --select "operation_retention or operation_web or operation_states or integrations or journal_interruption or backend_lifecycle"` passed 25 checks. Ruff flagged the test's sleep polling loop (ASYNC110); replaced polling with an event observing actual cleanup completion without changing the requirement. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot pytest tests/test_operation_retention.py -q` then passed all three retention tests; Ruff over reachy_brain/tests and diff checks passed. All 36 unique feature IDs and the specification hash remain intact.
- E1 remains partial. Main-database size plus the existing 8 KiB admission reserve does not yet prove the full total-storage requirement across update growth and SQLite temporary journal/VACUUM files; this is explicitly documented as unfinished qualification/implementation work. Other independent work includes supported-provider cancellation after dispatch, direct adapter/connection controls, mandatory-gate inventory and full-specification implementation audit. No physical, production connector or full V1 qualification is claimed.


### Build milestone: configuration-installed direct Python adapters

- Previous turn was progress (configured journal cleanup and retention evidence). IntegrationRuntime now selects an explicitly trusted Python factory for transport=python, alongside existing MCP transports. Disabled entries are not imported. The loader imports installed code off-thread, owns the adapter lifecycle, validates tool namespaces/catalog size and applies host policy separately. Configuration cannot relabel adapter writes as reads or remove adapter-declared scopes/capabilities/result limits.
- Added the test-only direct counter module and `examples/integrations.fake-direct.json`. This demonstrates read and confirmed write registration without core or composition edits. It uses synthetic accounts and transient state; no production account, persistent provider or restored-identity reconciliation claim. EXTENDING documents the factory/lifecycle contract, blocking-I/O expectations, host authorization and trusted-code boundary.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-direct-adapter --select "direct_adapter or runtime or integrations or mcp or backend_lifecycle"` passed 32 checks. Actual import/runtime tests cover discovery, validation, confirmation, duplicate execution, disabled tools, adapter closure, disabled-before-import, untrusted import rejection, action downgrade refusal and missing capabilities. Existing actual MCP transports and startup-failure cleanup remained passing.
- Review identified that arbitrary Rule.mode strings could otherwise fall through executor authorization branches. ActionPolicy.set now rejects unknown action/mode values. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-direct-adapter-reviewed --select "direct_adapter or runtime or integrations or mcp or backend_lifecycle"` passed 33 checks including invalid-policy rejection. Ruff and diff checks passed; all 36 unique IDs and the specification hash remain intact.
- E1 remains partial. Outstanding independent work includes provider cancellation after dispatch, complete connection/credential and persisted module/skill/standing-policy controls, peak journal disk bounds, mandatory-gate inventory and the full 36-feature implementation/acceptance audit. Direct adapters are trusted installed Python, not an execution sandbox for arbitrary skills. All mandatory dataset/provider and physical qualification remains separate.

### Build milestone: generated/heard assistant text and interruption records

- Previous turn was progress (bounded transcript provenance and 58 checks). Assistant records now retain generated user-facing text separately from acknowledged spoken text, with interruption and explicit generated-text truncation flags. Updates share the answer-start recording generation, stay on the bounded recorder queue and do not add unspoken text to conversation context. Metadata remains charged against transcript storage; its envelope grows to 64 KiB to accommodate bounded 12,000-character generated text with escaped Unicode.
- Core tracks text deltas, spoken question text and its local lookup-timeout explanation. Completion, heard acknowledgments, Stop and canceled answer cleanup update the owned answer record; old epochs cannot overwrite a newer answer's state. Stop after fully queued/acknowledged playback does not falsely mark that answer interrupted. UI/setup documentation now discloses that saved transcripts include generated text separately, replacing the earlier heard-only storage wording.

### Build milestone: session lifecycle and End ownership

- Previous turn was progress (transcript provenance and 193 implemented offline checks). Added structured session lifecycle records in the same bounded SQLite entry store, distinguished from conversational turns and included in explicit exports. Session lists expose lifecycle metadata and count conversational turns separately.

Answer records bind their originating session, and cleanup after a session replacement cannot overwrite it or write into the replacement. Mode changes, active opt-in, turns, evidence clears and robot failure paths capture applicable lifecycle snapshots. Transcript-off remains a hard admission boundary: a missing end is unknown, not fabricated. Crash recovery does not invent an end time.

### Build milestone: source timing through recognition commits

- Previous turn was progress (session lifecycle and 47 affected checks). Recognition commits now snapshot available source onset/end intervals with their original opt-in generation; committed provider item IDs retain those snapshots through out-of-order transcription completions. Unknown or invalid intervals are omitted, never replaced with transcript arrival time. A consumed onset cannot be reused by a later manual commit.

The backend bounds and validates timing markers. Robot speech-end markers use the existing edge clock mapping only when fresh and within its 100 ms uncertainty limit. ### Build milestone: separately authorized provider cancellation dispatch

- Previous turn was progress (MCP HTTP authentication and 18 checks). Re-read SPEC section 16's explicit provider-cancellation requirement and E1 lifecycle/reconciliation gates. Added a cancel-tool relationship and executor request path bound to the original module/account identity and operation. A request requires a distinct operation ID and runs through existing per-tool policy, exact confirmation, dispatch and durable duplicate handling. MCP configuration can declare the relationship; direct Tool instances can supply it.
- Cancellation is a separately journaled external write. Only dispatched/uncertain original operations qualify; missing support, disabled access or replaced account identity cannot dispatch. A successful request receipt does not mark the original undone. Original outcome remains available for its own completion/reconciliation. Speech Stop remains independent and does not call this path.

### Build milestone: separate workflow and retrieval budgets

- Previous turn was progress: live confirmed/standing-authorized writes over both transports and 29 affected offline checks. Inspected actual controller limits against SPEC retrieval requirements. Mixed workflows cannot reset retrieval time or rounds; expired retrieval produces an explicit no-dispatch limit result. Overall workflow exhaustion stops further tools with a bounded explanation.

Its waiting time pauses only the workflow work deadline; after approval, visual/document calls recheck the original retrieval deadline and cannot dispatch if it expired. Approval does not change account/tool/payload policy checks. An initial patch failed to match formatter-adjusted context; no edit was applied by that failed patch, and the subsequent context-correct patch succeeded.

### Build milestone: bounded usage checkpoints across backend restart

- Previous turn was progress: separate workflow/retrieval limits, 245 implemented offline checks and five live workflow checks. Reinspected actual ProviderGate and application resource acquisition/shutdown before adding persistence.
- Added a validated maximum-4-MiB checkpoint for aggregate known estimates, unknown-charge count and at most 10,000 provider/request identities. Detailed usage payloads, speech and transcripts are not persisted. A single coalescing async worker snapshots counters and atomically writes/fsyncs/replaces the file off-thread. Recording usage signals the worker without disk I/O; shutdown flushes after provider/conversation cleanup. Diagnostics expose pending/saved revisions and bounded errors.
- Startup restores numeric-budget admission and deduplication. Corrupt checkpoints are not overwritten/reset; failed writes block new provider work and leave immediate local Stop independent. The previous checkpoint survives replacement failure and temporary files are cleaned up. These are asynchronous checkpoints, not crash-proof exactly-once billing records; a crash can lose pending revisions.
- Five new synthetic checks cover restored cost/unknown totals, duplicate suppression after restart, persisted numeric-budget denial, real application lifespan/authorized status restoration, blocked disk with actual controller Stop, later-revision coalescing, atomic replacement failure and corruption. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-usage-checkpoints-final --select "usage_storage or costs or backend_lifecycle or resources or voice_selection"` passed 18 affected checks. Ruff/diff checks passed; all 36 IDs and unchanged SPEC hash verified. One patch did not match formatter-adjusted test context; no changes applied from it, and the corrected patch succeeded.
- Deployment documentation now distinguishes saved aggregate history from process-only recent rows and unavailable historical usage. Remaining accounting work includes concurrent-writer ownership, identity archival/rotation at capacity, crash/admission reservations, active-session reporting, provider-specific speech rates and representative cost measurements. Use one backend per data directory until ownership is enforced. No complete spending cap/accounting tier or physical qualification is claimed. The full specification/acceptance audit and other outstanding implementation/evaluation requirements remain active.

### Build milestone: exclusive accounting writer ownership

- Previous turn was progress: bounded usage checkpoints and 18 affected checks. Inspected actual checkpoint load/write/shutdown paths and closed the concurrent-writer gap. The backend now acquires a permanent sidecar OS lock before loading totals and holds it through its final write. Windows uses nonblocking byte-range locking; POSIX uses nonblocking flock. A contending writer fails startup without loading/replacing the owned checkpoint. The sidecar is not deleted, avoiding separate lock files for competing writers.
- Acquisition and release run off-thread. A dedicated cleanup task joins in-flight acquisition and final writes before releasing ownership, even when startup/shutdown callers receive cancellation more than once. Corrupt loads release their acquired lock; write failures retain ownership until cleanup. Process termination releases the OS lock without manual stale-lock deletion.
- Four new scenarios use actual Windows process contention, normal close, forced termination and deterministic acquisition/write barriers. Initial process tests failed only because the readiness assertion expected LF while Windows emitted CRLF; retained stage-usage-ownership failure evidence and corrected line-ending handling without changing ownership assertions. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-usage-ownership-final --select "usage_ownership or usage_storage or backend_lifecycle or costs"` passed 14 affected checks. Tests verify the checkpoint is unchanged during contention, restored counters advance after takeover, and canceled cleanup never releases ownership before pending I/O finishes.
- Documentation now states enforced single-writer behavior and leaves POSIX/Reachy filesystem qualification explicit. Ruff/diff checks passed; all 36 IDs and the unchanged specification hash were verified. No billable calls or account changes were made. Pending-checkpoint crash loss, admission reservations, identity archival/rotation, active-session reporting and provider-rate coverage remain separate accounting work; full specification/acceptance and physical qualification remain active.

- Full implemented regression: `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-accounting-ownership-regression` passed all 254 currently implemented offline checks after checkpoint and ownership changes. This is not proof that every mandatory gate is implemented or that live/physical tiers passed; feature tiers remain unpromoted.

### Build milestone: bounded canonical conversation summaries

- Previous turn made progress on speech-failure races. Reviewed C8 and found history used a 40-message deque without summaries. Added Astra compaction before answers when history exceeds 24 messages or 24,000 characters, retaining the newest eight messages verbatim and bounding derived summary output to 3,000 characters/ten seconds. Input contains canonical user and heard-assistant history only; generated-but-unheard text is excluded. No summary tools or opaque provider response chains are used. Pending gesture messages are protected from compaction.
- Cancellation closes the provider stream. Epoch/history identity checks fence late results, including visual clear and Stop. Summary failure preserves existing bounded history and reports unavailable; successful compaction is labeled derived memory, not a new user instruction/permission. ### Build milestone: bounded parser subprocess output

- Previous turn made progress on complete project coverage totals. Found parser stdout was capped only after subprocess.run had collected it and stderr was unbounded. Replaced capture_output with a bounded stdout-draining reader, immediate child termination on excess bytes, discarded stderr and minimized child environment without provider credentials. Timeout/failure returns no partial passages; process termination/reaping and reader joining complete before parser-slot release. Unexpected caller exceptions also terminate the direct child. The trusted parser does not spawn descendants inheriting its pipe.
- Added six real hidden native-process cases: exact output limit, continuous overflow, timeout, nonzero exit, secret-environment/stderr isolation and a single excess byte followed by a sleeping child. The last case proves over-limit detection does not wait for EOF or a full read buffer. A patch initially failed to match formatter-adjusted test context; no edits from that patch applied, and the corrected patch succeeded.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-parser-capture-final --select "parser_process or k1_corpus or k1_worker_races"` passed 12 checks including the corpus and removal/cancellation races. ### Build milestone: native parser-child memory limits

- Previous turn made progress on bounded parser output. Added a bootstrap used by ProjectIndex that applies a 512 MiB child-process memory ceiling before importing extraction libraries. Windows uses a Job Object per-process committed-memory limit via documented kernel APIs; Linux uses RLIMIT_AS while preserving lower inherited limits. Only the disposable child applies limits; no parent-process limit is changed. Unsupported/failed setup returns resource_limits_unavailable without importing/executing extraction. MemoryError returns over_limit.
- Verified current official Microsoft extended-job/SetInformationJobObject contracts and Python resource documentation; linked distinctions in UPSTREAM.md. Windows committed memory and Linux address space are not equivalent to physical RSS. The Windows job handle is retained for the child lifetime and released by process exit. Existing stdout/time/concurrency/environment limits remain.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-parser-memory --select "parser_memory or parser_process or k1_corpus or k1_worker_races"` passed 14 checks. A real native Windows child with a 64 MiB limit could allocate 1 MiB but received MemoryError for 128 MiB; the full corpus also parsed under the production 512 MiB ceiling. A separate child verifies failed limit setup never imports document libraries. No mocked OS enforcement was counted as a native pass.
- Ruff/diff checks passed; all 36 feature IDs and unchanged SPEC hash verified. Updated deployment/tracker evidence without promoting whole-feature tiers. Linux/Reachy resource enforcement, representative peak-resource/soak qualification, unchanged-file error retry behavior and remaining application/eval requirements remain outstanding. No provider calls or physical media/motion tests occurred.

### Build milestone: recorded E1 integration interruption evaluation

### Build milestone: independently recorded ordinary-response latency

- Previous turn completed archival frame admission and its 14-check regression. Re-read current acceptance gates and identified that backend response timings do not establish speech-end-to-audible-output latency. Added a physical response scorer and required live-PC manifest check, preserving the ordinary median <3 s / p95 <5 s targets and separate retrieval results. No application/provider configuration changed.
- Score bounded, hash-verified independent PCM with a reviewed user endpoint, calibrated isolated assistant channel and explicit alignment uncertainty. Use the end of the first active 5 ms RMS window plus uncertainty. Reject absent audio, pre-endpoint assistant energy, bad calibration, wrong channels, truncation and unreviewed evidence. Keep both provider paths and actual fallback separate; report count/median/p95/slowest. Human review must establish first energy belongs to the actual answer, not a click/user leak. The scorer cannot authenticate capture or review declarations.
- New harness baseline `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select response_acoustics --output local-data/evidence/stage-response-acoustics-before` failed collection because the scorer module did not yet exist. ### Build milestone: playback queue isolation across epochs

- Found that accepted fresh worklet authorization changed the epoch without clearing pending samples or completion markers. If authorization arrived while the previous queue remained, old audio could play and old markers could be acknowledged under the new epoch. Added a deterministic shipped-worklet Chromium reproduction first: `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_new_epoch_cannot_play' --output local-data/evidence/stage-worklet-epoch-queue-before` failed the queue-isolation assertion. The retained failure records the original behavior.
- Accepted fresh authorization now clears the queue and buffered sample accounting before setting the new epoch. Invalid authorization preserves current playback state. Confirmed SpeechPipeline authorizes once per epoch rather than per segment. The new check verifies old and late-old samples/markers cannot play or produce heard acknowledgments, while new-epoch playback and completion remain functional.

### Build milestone: transcript export worker isolation

- C9 review found that transcript JSON serialization, text assembly and response-byte encoding ran on the application event loop after the database read. Moved the full export snapshot/read/render path to a worker. Two export slots bound concurrent snapshot/render work; additional requests receive 429 without loading another transcript. HTTP cancellation is shielded from the owned worker, so capacity is returned only on actual completion. Late worker errors are observed. Existing authenticated UUID/format validation, missing-session 404, JSON shape, text content, UTF-8 encoding and no-store/nosniff/download headers remain intact.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_transcript_export_workers or test_transcript_recording or test_transcript_evidence or test_transcript_snapshot' --output local-data/evidence/stage-transcript-export-lifecycle` passed 15 checks with none failed, blocked or unexecuted. Actual app lifespan and private HTTP tests hold both worker threads, verify rendering occurs off the event-loop thread, serve a separate settings endpoint while held, enforce busy admission after canceling one HTTP request, then release and validate both export formats plus capacity recovery after a missing-session error. Existing transcript controls, deletion/evidence and immutable recorder metadata checks also passed. Fixtures contain synthetic saved text; no physical interruption or provider claim.
- Ruff passed. This closes the synchronous export-rendering finding, not full C9 acceptance or physical audio timing. Documentation and C2/C9/D5 evidence updated without whole-feature promotion. The retained wheel predates this runtime fix and will need final refresh. Remaining full-scope review and mandatory physical/product/soak/robot qualification continue.


### Build milestone: pending transcript export privacy fence

- Reproduced a pending-export race: both JSON and text could deliver an already rendered private snapshot after scoped deletion, delete-all or disabling recording had completed. `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_pending_export_rejects_changed_transcript_state' --output local-data/evidence/stage-export-generation-before` failed all six scenarios with HTTP 200 instead of rejecting the stale response. This failed evidence is retained.
- Export admission now rejects an in-progress transcript state transition. It captures the recorder generation and rechecks generation/transition state when the worker returns, before handing the response to HTTP delivery. A changed generation returns 409 without the snapshot. The worker-capacity/cancellation rules remain intact. A fresh export still works after disabling recording because saved data was not deleted; deleted sessions remain 404. This cannot retract bytes already delivered to a client and does not promise cancellation of an external copy.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_transcript_export_workers or test_transcript_recording or test_transcript_evidence or test_transcript_snapshot' --output local-data/evidence/stage-export-generation-fixed` passed 21 checks with none failed, blocked or unexecuted. Tests use actual app lifespan/SQLite/private HTTP with barriers after response rendering, verify deletion completes independently, reject stale private text in both formats and verify fresh-export behavior. Ruff and git diff --check passed. C9/D5 evidence updated without feature promotion. Full offline regression started separately; no provider or physical-device call was needed.

- Full regression completed: `uv run --extra vision --extra robot iago-verify --suite offline --output local-data/evidence/stage-export-privacy-full-regression` selected 823 checks: 821 passed, zero failed, two blocked, none unexecuted. ### Build milestone: motion excursion correction

- Reviewed the recorded motion scorer and reproduced a false negative: two poses on opposite sides of the first pose can each fit its uncertainty margin while their separation exceeds the allowed measurement uncertainty. Replaced reference-to-first displacement with maximum pairwise separation, using full translation ranges and bounded O(n log n) angular wraparound calculations. The original lease and two-error threshold remain unchanged.
- `uv run --extra vision --extra robot --extra robot-camera iago-verify --suite offline --select 'test_opposite_excursions or test_angular_excursion' --output local-data/evidence/stage-motion-pairwise-before` retained three failures and three passes.
